### T000c9ff9cc6f60b766efcd58

Text hash 0ac7475a228b70a0c08914a28fd290a59d3ecf1dd102f7b3bb9ac43cc6362d94; absolute characters 0–373; files: jobs/source-ladder/output/texts/queue_EO_10048_Actual_report_date_of_the_board_created_by_EO_10048_Presidential_Emergency_Board_Rail_6ee4f2ba5b.txt

```text
Wanted: queue:EO:10048:Actual report date of the board created by EO 10048 (Presidential Emergency Board (Railway Labor Act sec. 10): the South ern Pacific Company (Pacific Lines ) and Certain of Its Employees): the board's report to the President and its transmittal letter date; not the order's report deadline, and the 1986 revocation by EO 12553 is only an upper bound.

### T02d5cf41d4ce538acb0384cf

In designating its members, the President instructed the Board 
to "investigate promptly the facts as to such dispute and, on the 
basis of the facts developed, make every effort to adjust the dis- 
pute and report thereon to me within 30 days from the date of the 
Executive order." The Board has given careful consideration to 
the possibility of seeking an adjustment of this dispute through a 
mediated settlement acceptable to the parties.
```

VOLUME 14 ^ O y V r r E O ^  - NUMBER 74
Washington, Tuesday, April 19, 1949
TITLE 3— THE PRESIDENT  
PROCLAMATION 2835  
Child  H ealth D ay, 1949
BY THE PRESIDENT OF THE UNITED STATES  
OF AM ERICA 
A  PROCLAMATION
WHEREAS the Congress, by joint 
resolution of May 18,1928 (45 Stat. 617),, 
has authorized and requested the Presi­
dent to issue annually a proclamation 
setting apart May 1  as Child Health 
Day; and
WHEREAS every citizen should do his 
utmost toward safeguarding and improv­
ing the health of the Nation’ s children:
NOW, THEREFORE, I, HARRY S. 
TRUMAN, President of the United 
States of America, do hereby designate 
May 1, 1949, as Child Health Day; and 
I invite all agencies, organizations, and 
citizens interested in the physical and 
mental well-being of children to con­
sider on that day how best to promote in 
their own communities during the com­
ing year definite programs of action de­
signed to help our children to grow into 
healthy and responsible individuals 
dedicated to the principles of democracy.
IN WITNESS WHEREOF, I have here­
unto set my hand and caused the Seal 
of the^ United States of America to be 
affixed.
DONE at the City of Washington this 
16th day of April in the year of our Lord 
nineteen hundred and forty- 
Cse a l] nine, and of thè Independence 
of the United States of America 
the one hundred and seventy-third.
H arRy S. T ruman
By the President;
D ean A cheson ,
Secretary of-State.
[P. R. Doc. 49-3102; Piled, Apr. 18, 1949;
11:22 a. m .j
EXECUTIVE ORDER 10051
Creating  an E mergency  B oard T o I nves ­
tigate  a D ispute  B etween  the  A l i- 
quippa  and S outhern  R ailroad C o m­
pany  and C ertain  of I ts E mployees
WHEREAS a dispute exists between 
the Aliquippa and Southern Railroad
Company, a carrier, and certain of its 
employees represented by the Brother­
hood of Railroad Trainmen, a labor or­
ganization; and
WHEREAS this dispute has not here­
tofore been adjusted under the provi­
sions of the Railway Labor Act, as 
amended; and
WHEREAS this dispute, in the judg­
ment of the National Mediation Board, 
threatens substantially to interrupt in­
terstate commerce within the State of 
Pennsylvania to a degree such as to de­
prive that portion of the country of es- 
serftial transportation service:
NOW, THEREFORE, by virtue of the 
authority vested in me by section 10 of 
the Railway Labor Act, as amended (45 
U. S. C. 160), I hereby create a board of 
three members, to be appointed by me, to 
investigate the said dispute. No mem­
ber of the said board shall be pecuniarily 
or otherwise interested in any organiza­
tion of railway employees or any carrier.
The board shall report its findings to 
the President with respect to the said 
dispute within thirty days from the date 
of this order.
As provided by section 10 of the Rail­
way Labor Act, as amended, from this 
date and for thirty days after the board 
has made its report to the President, no 
change, except by agreement, shall be 
made by the Aliquippa and Southern 
Railroad Company or its employees in 
the conditions out of which the said dis­
pute arose.
H arry S. T ruman
T he  W h it e H ouse ,
April 15, 1949.
[F. R. Doc. 49-3085; Filed, Apr. 15, 1949;
5:08 p. m.]

v o iu i"  “ NUMBER 20
Washington, Tuesday, February 1, 1949
TITLE 3— THE PRESIDENT
EXECUTIVE ORDER 10032
Creating an E mergency B oard to I nves­
tigate a D ispute  B etween  the A kron, 
Canton &  Youngstown  R ailroad C om­
pany  and O ther C arriers, and C ertain 
of T heir E mployees
WHEREAS a dispute exists between 
the Akron, Canton ty  Youngstown Rail­
road Company and certain other carriers 
designated in the list attached hereto 
and made a part hereof, and certain of 
their employees represented by the 
Brotherhood of Locomotive Engineers, a 
labor organization; and 
WHEREAS this dispute has not here-' 
tofore been adjusted under the provi­
sions of the Railway Labor Act, as 
amended; and
WHEREAS this dispute, in the judg­
ement of the National Mediation Board, 
threatens substantially* to interrupt in­
terstate commerce to a degree such as 
to deprive the country of essential trans­
portation service:
NOW, THEREFORE, by virtue of the 
authority vested in me by section 10 of 
the Railway Labor Act, as amended (45 
U. S. C. 160), I hereby create a board 
of three members, to be appointed by 
me, to investigate the said dispute. No 
member of the said board shall be pe­
cuniarily or otherwise interested in any 
organization of employees or any carrier.
The board shall report its findings to 
the President with respect to the said 
dispute within thirty days from the date of this order.
As provided by section 10 of the Rail­
way Labor Act, as amended, from this 
date and for thirty days after the board 
has made its report to the President, no 
change, except by agreement, shall be 
made by any of the carriers involved or 
its employees in the conditions out of 
which the said dispute arose.
H arry S. T ruman
The W hite  H ouse,
January 28, 1949.

cial goal among the counties as

age goals of three or more acres for

cial farms.

.  three acres or more for new farms.

648.80 .  Goal for non-commercial farm. *

9  GENERAL
§  648.71 Applicability of  §§648.71 to 
648.85.' Sections 648.71 to 648.85 shall 
govern the establishment of farm acre­
age goals for Irish potatoes for use in 
connection with United States Depart­
ment of Agriculture price support opera­
tions relating to the 1949 crop of such 
potatoes. The planting of potatoes in ex­
cess of the 1949 goal established for any 
farm, or, where separate goals are estab­
lished for early and late potatoes, the 
planting or potatoes in excess of either 
of such goals, shall, subsequent to the 
date of such excess planting, render any 
person having an interest in such farm
as operator, owner, landlord, tenant or 
partner ineligible to participate in 1949 
potato price support operations. Such 
ineligibility shall extend also to any cor­
poration or corporate stockholder whose 
operations are subject to substantially 
the same management, ownership, or 
control as those of a corporation or cor­
porate stockholder planting potatoes in 
excess of an acreage goal. Potatoes 
which are produced on a farm owned or 
operated by a partnership shall be in­
eligible in case any partner plants pota­
toes on any other farm in excess of the 
acreage goal for such farm. The entire 
interest in potatoes planted within a 
non-commercial farm goal must be in the 
owner, or in the owner and the operator, 
of such entire farm. Any person having 
an interest, other than as owner or opera­
tor of the entire farm, in potato produc­
tion from one or more non-commercial 
farms, shall be ineligible to participate 
in 1949 potato price support operations. 
Publicly owned experiment stations 
planting potatoes for experimental pur­
poses only do not come within the scope 
of this program; no goal shall be estab­
lished for such experimental plantings 
nor shall the potatoes produced thereon 
be eligible for acquisition under price 
support operations.
§  648.72 Definitions. As used in 
§§ 648.71 to 648.85 and in all instructions, 
forms, and documents in connection 
therewith, the words and phrases defined 
in this section shall have the meanings 
herein assigned to them unless the con­
text or subject matter requires otherwise.
(a) Committees: (1) “County com­
mittee” means the group of persons 
elected within a county to assist in the 
administration of the Agricultural Con­
servation Program in such county.
(2) “State committee” means the 
group of persons designated as the State 
committee of the Production and Market­
ing Administration charged with the re­
sponsibility of administering Production 
and Marketing Administration programs 
within the State.
(b) “Farm” means ah' adjacent or 
nearby farm land under the same owner­
ship which is operated by one person, 
including also: (1) Any other adjacent 
or nearby farm land which the county 
committee, in accordance with instruc­
tions issued by the Production and Mar­
keting Administration, determines is op­
erated by the same persons as part of 
the same unit with respect to rotation of 
crops and with workstock, farm machin­
ery and labor substantially separate from 
that for any other lands;* and (2) any 
field-rented tract (whether operated by
the same or another person) which, to­
gether with any other land included in 
the farm, constitutes a unit with respect 
to the rotation of crops. A farm shall 
be regarded as located in the county in 
which the principal dwelling is situated, 
or if there is no dwelling thereon it shall 
be regarded as located in the county in 
which the major portion of the farm is 
located.
(c) “New farm” means a farm on 
which potatoes have not been planted 
since 1945 and on which the 1949 farm 
operator’ s personal history of potato pro­
duction since 1945 is not used in deter­
mining the farm goal.
(d) “Old farm” means a farm on 
which potatoes have been planted in one 
or more years since 1945 or on which the 
1949 farm operator’ s personal history of 
potato production since 1945 is used in 
determining the farm goal.
(e) “Commercial farm” means a farm 
for which a preliminary 1949 goal of 
three or more acres is established.
(f) “Non-commercial farm” meçms a 
farm on which potatoes are planted in 
1949 and for which a preliminary 1949 
potato acreage goal of three or more 
acres is not established.
(g) “National goal” means the total 
number of acres of Irish potatoes estab­
lished by the United States Department 
of Agriculture as necessary to produce a 
1949 crop sufficient for all domestic and 
export requirements without unduly cur­
tailing production in any area.
(h) “State goal” means the number of 
acres out of the National goal allocated 
by the Production and Marketing Ad­
ministration to be planted within a par­
ticular State.
(i) “Commercial goal” means that part 
of the total goal which is made available 
for apportioning to commercial farms.
(j) “Non-commercial goal” means that 
part of the total goal which is attributed 
to non-commercial farms. No direct ap­
portionment of the non-commercial goal 
to any farm is provided.
(k) “Preliminary goal” is the acreage 
which the county committee preliminar­
ily determines to be the proper goal for a 
farm, in view of the county limit as here­
inafter defined, past goals on the farm, 
topography, crop rotation practice, and 
land, labor, and equipment available for 
the production, handling, and market­
ing of potatoes grown on the farm.
(l) “Final goal” for a farm Is its pre­
liminary goal after such goal has been 
adjusted by the county committee so that 
the total of all final goals in the county 
shall not exceed the county limit, pur­
suant to the procedure specified in 
§§ 648.73 and 648.78.

FEDERAL REGISTER
VOLUME 14 NUMBER 14• V ,  1 9 3 4<  tJNnto ^
Washington, Thursday, January 20, 1949
TITLE 3— THE PRESIDENT
EXECUTIVE ORDER 10029
Creating an E mergency B oard to I nves­
tigate a D ispute  B etween  the N orth­
west  Airlines , I nc., and Certain of Its 
Employees
WHEREAS a dispute exists between 
the Northwest Airlines, Inc., a carrier, 
and certain of its employees represented 
by the International Association of Ma­
chinists, a labor organization; and 
WHEREAS this dispute has not here­
tofore been adjusted under the provi­
sions of the Railway Labor Act, as 
amended; and
WHEREAS this dispute, in the judg­
ment of the National Mediation Board, 
threatens substantially to interrupt in­
terstate commerce to a degree such as 
to deprive a large section of the country 
of essential transportation service:
NOW, THEREFORE by virtue of the 
authority vested in me by section 10 of 
the Railway Labor Act, as amended (45
U. S. C. 160), I hereby create a board of 
three members, to be appointed by me, 
to investigate the said dispute. No mem­
ber of the said board shall be pecuniarily 
or otherwise interested in any organiza­
tion of employees or any carrier.
The board shall report its findings to 
the President with respect to the said 
dispute within thirty days from the date 
of this order.
As provided by section 10 of the Rail­
way Labor Act, as amended, from this 
date and for thirty days after the board 
has made its report to the President, no 
change, except by agreement, shall be 
made by the Northwest Airlines, Inc., or 
its employees in the conditions out of 
which the said dispute arose.
H arry S. T ruman
The W hite  H ouse,
January 19, 1949.

EXECUTIVE ORDER 10009
R evoking in  P art E xecutive O rders N o. 
589 of M arch 14,1907, and N o. 1712 of 
F ebruary 24, 1913
WHEREAS Executive Order No. 589 of 
March 14,1907, recited in part that pass­
ports issued by the Government of Ja­
pan to Japanese or Korean citizens who 
were skilled or unskilled laborers to go 
to Mexico, Canada, or Hawaii 'were be­
ing used for the purpose of enabling the 
holders thereof to come to the conti­
nental territory of the United States to 
the detriment of labor conditions there­
in, and ordered that such citizens of Ja­
pan or Korea be refused permission to 
enter the continental territory of the 
United States and 
WHEREAS Executive Order No. 1712 
of February 24, 1913, recited that pass­
ports issued by certain foreign govern­
ments to their citizens or subjects, skilled 
or unskilled laborers, to go to countries 
or places other than the continental ter­
ritory of the United States were being 
used for the purpose of enabling the 
holders thereof to come to the x  conti­
nental territory of the United States to
the detriment of labor conditions there­
in, and ordered that such alien laborers, 
skilled or unskilled, be refused permis­
sion to enter the continental territory 
of the United States; and
WHEREAS the continued restraint 
imposed by the said Executive orders 
upon the freedom of Japanese or Korean 
citizens or subjects who entered the Ter­
ritory of Hawaii under the passports 
referred to above to come to the con­
tinental United States has resulted in 
considerable personal hardship to such 
persons; and
WHEREAS the number of Japanese 
or Korean citizens or subjects thus pro­
hibited from entering the continental 
United States from the Territory of Ha­
waii is so small, and the age of such 
persons is so advanced, that their entry 
into the continental United States would 
no longer be a detriment to labor con­
ditions therein:
NOW, THEREFORE, by virtue of the 
authority vested in me by section 3 of the 
Immigration Act of 1917 (39 Stat. 875; 
8 U. S. C. 136), I hereby revoke the said 
Executive Orders No. 589 of March 14, 
1907, and No. 1712 of February 24, 1913, 
to the extent that they are applicable 
to Japanese or Korean citizens or sub­
jects who entered the Territory of Hawaii 
under the aforesaid passports and who 
are now precluded from legally enterr 
ing or remaining in the continental ter­
ritory of the United States by reason of 
such Executive orders.
The Commissioner of Immigration and 
Naturalization is hereby authorized and 
directed, with the approval of the At­
torney General, to take such action and 
to issue such rules and regulations as 
may be necessary for carrying out the 
provisions of this order.
H arry S. Truman
The W hite H ouse,
October 18, 1948.
[F. R. Doc. 48-9261, Filed Oct. 18, 1948;
11:50 a. m.]
EXECUTIVE ORDER 10010
Creating an E mergency B oard to I nves­
tigate D isputes  B etween  the A kron 
&  Barberton B elt R ailroad C ompany 
and O ther C arriers, and C ertain of 
Their  E mployees
WHEREAS disputes exist between the 
Akron &  Barberton Belt Railroad Com­
pany and certain other carriers desig­
nated in List A attached hereto and made 
a part hereof, and certain of their em­
ployees represented by the sixteen co­
operating railway labor organizations 
designated in List B attached hereto and 
made a part hereof; and 
WHEREAS these disputes have not 
heretofore been adjusted under the pro­
visions jof the Railway Labor Act, as 
amended; and
WHEREAS these disputes, in the judg­
ment of the National Mediation Board, 
threaten substantially to interrupt in­
terstate commerce to a degree such as 
to deprive the country of essential trans­
portation service:
NOW, THEREFORE, by virtue of the 
authority vested in me by section 10 of 
the Railway Labor Act, as amended (45 
U. S. C. 160), I hereby create a board of 
three members, to be appointed by me, to 
investigate the said disputes.
No member of the said board shall be 
pecuniarily or otherwise interested in 
any organization of railway employees 
or any carrier.
The board shall report its findings to 
the President with respect to the said 
disputes within thirty days from the date 
of this order.
As provided by section 10 of the Rail­
way Labor Act, as amended, from this 
date and for thirty days after the board 
has made its report to the President, no 
change, except by agreement, shall be 
made by any of the carriers involved or 
its employees in the conditions out of 
which the said disputes arose.
Harry S. T ruman
T he W hite H ouse,
October 18,1948.

FEDERAL REGSTER
VOLUME 13 1934* V MitE O  ^ NUMBER 219
Washington, Tuesday, November 9, 1948
TITLE 3— THE PRESIDENT
EXECUTIVE ORDER 10014  «
Directing Federal Agencies to Cooperate 
W ith S tate and L ocal A uthorities  in  
Preventing P ollution  of Surface and 
Underground W aters
By virtue of the authority vested in me 
as President of the United States, and 
pursuant to the policy expressed, in sec­
tion 1  of the Water Pollution Control 
Act approved June 30, 1948 (Public Law 
845, 80th Congress), of recognizing, pre­
serving, and protecting the primary re­
sponsibilities and rights of the States in 
controlling water pollution, I  hereby di­
rect the heads of the departments, agen­
cies, and independent establishments of 
the executive branch of the Government 
to take such action as may be practicable, 
in cooperation with State and local au­
thorities concerned with control of water 
'pollution, to insure the disposal of sew­
age, garbage, refuse, and other wastes 
accuinulated in the course or as ^ r e ­
sult of Federal activities, and industrial 
or manufactured foodstuffs and 'other 
products destroyed by order or under the 
supervision of Federal regulatory au­
thorities, in such manner as will con­
form with programs formulated under 
State law and applicable to Stat agen­
cies and the public generally for the pres­
ervation and improvement of the qual­
ity of surface and underground waters.
Harry S. T ruman
The W hite H ouse,
November 3,1948.
[F. R. Doc. 48-9872; Filed, Nov. 5, 1948;
2:26 p. m.]
EXECUTIVE ORDER 10015
Amendment of E xecutive O rder N o. 
10010, Creating an E mergency B oard 
To Investigate Disputes  B etween  the 
Akron & B arberton B elt R ailroad 
Company  and Other Carriers, and Cer­
tain of T heir E mployees
By virtue of authority vested in me by 
section 10 of the Railway Labor Act, as 
amended (45 H  S. C. 160), Executive Or­
der No. 10010 of October 18, 1948, en­
titled “Creating an Emergency Board to
Investigate Disputes between the Akron 
& Barberton Belt Railroad Company and 
Other Carriers, and Certain of Their Em­
ployees,”  is hereby amended as follows:
1 . The following-named carriers are 
hereby added to the list of carriers com­
prising List A attached to and made a 
part of the said Executive order;
The New York & Long Branch Railroad  
Company
Wharton & Northern Railroad
Jersey Central Transportation Company
Grand Central Terminals
State University Railroad Company
Woodstock & Blockton Railway Company
2. The railway labor organizations des­
ignated in List B attached to and made 
a part of the said Executive order as 
“Brotherhood of Railway Carmen of 
America” and “Hotel and Restaurant 
Employes* International Alliance and 
Bartenders’ Int. League of America” are 
hereby designated respectively as “Broth­
erhood Railway Carmen of America” and 
“Hotel & Restaurant Employees and Bar- 
tenders’ International Union.”
This order shall be effective as of Octo­
ber 18, 1948.
Harry S. T ruman
The W hite House,
November 5,1948.
[F. R. Doc. 48-9888; Filed, Nov. 8, 1948;
11:17 a. m.j

pute, the Board found it necessary to request of the President, 
by letter dated February 14, 1949, an extension of 45 days from 
February 27 as the time within which its repor~ was to be made~ 
The parties to the proceeding stipulated their approval of this 
request. 1 Acting upon the recommendation of the National Medi- 
ation Board, the President on February 19, 1949, approved the 
extension request "permitting this Emergency Board to file its re- 
port and recommendations not later than April 13, 1949. ''2 
The hearings of the Board were held from February 7, 1949, 
to February 24, 1949, (inclusive) and from March 14, 1949, to 
March 23, 1949, (inclusive).

Washington, Saturday, May 14, 1949
TITLE 3— THE PRESIDENT
EXECUTIVE ORDER 10056
Creating an E mergency  B oard to I nves ­
tigate a D ispute  B etw een the  U nion
R ailroad C ompany  (P ittsburgh ) and
Certain  qF I ts E mployees
WHEREAS a dispute exists between 
the Union Railroad Company (Pitts­
burgh) , a carrier, and certain of its em­
ployees represented by the Brotherhood 
of Railroad Trainmen, a labor organiza­
tion; and
WHEREAS this dispute has not here­
tofore been adjusted under the provi­
sions of the Railway Labor Act, as 
amended; and
WHEREAS this dispute, in the judg­
ment of the National Mediation Board, 
threatens substantially to interrupt in­
terstate commerce within the State of 
Pennsylvania to a degree such as to de­
prive that portion of the country of 
essential transportation service:
NOW, THEREFORE, by virtue of the 
authority vested in me by section 10 .of- 
the Railway Labor Act, as amended (45 
U. S. C. 160), I hereby create a board of 
three members, to be appointed by me, 
to investigate the said dispute. No 
member of the said board shall be pe­
cuniarily or otherwise interested in any 
organization of railway employees or any 
carrier.
The board shall report its findings to 
the President with respect to the said 
dispute within thirty days from the date 
of this order.
As provided by section 10 of the Rail­
way Labor Act, as amended, from this 
date and for thirty days after the board 
has made its report to the President, no 
change, except by agreement, shall be 
made by the Union Railroad Company 
(Pittsburgh) orbits employees in the con­
ditions out of which the said dispute 
arose.
H arry  S. T ruman
T he W hite  H ouse ,
May 12,1949.
IP. R. Doc. 49-3926; Filed, May 13, 1949;
10:05 a. m.]

LIMITATION OF SHIPMENTS
§  953.426 Lemon Regulation 319 —(a) 
Findings. (1) Pursuant to the market­
ing agreement, as amended, and Order 
No. 53, as amended (7 CFR, Cum. Supp., 
953.1 et seq.; 13 F. R. 766), regulating 
the handling of lemons grown in the 
State of California or in the State of 
Arizona, effective under the applicable 
provisions of the Agricultural Marketing 
Agreement Act of 1937, as amended, and 
upon the basis of the recommendation 
and information submitted by the Lemon 
Administrative Committee, established 
under the said amended marketing 
agreement and order, and upon other 
available information, it is hereby found 
that the limitation of the quantity of 
such lemons which may be handled, as 
hereinafter provided, will tend to effec­
tuate the declared policy of the act.
(2) It is hereby further found that it 
is impracticable and contrary to the 
public interest to give preliminary notice, 
engage in public rulemaking procedure, 
and postpone the effective date of this 
section until 30 days after publication 
thereof in the F ederal R egister  (60 Stat. 
237; 5 U. S. C. 1001 et seq.) because the 
time intervening between the date when 
information upon which this section Is 
based became available and the time 
when this section must become effective 
in order to effectuate the declared policy 
of the Agricultural Marketing Agreement 
Act of 1937, as amended, is insufficient, 
and a reasonable time is permitted, under 
the circumstances, for preparation for 
such effective date.
(b) Order. (1) The quantity of lem­
ons grown in the State of California or 
in the State of Arizona which may be 
handled during the period beginning at 
12:01 a. m., P. s. t., May 15, 1949, and

- W A U .
DERAL STER
VOLUME 14 ^ U / t u j E O * ^ NUMBER 62
• Washington, Friday, April 1, 1949
TITLE 3— THE PRESIDENT
EXECUTIVE ORDER 10048
Creating an E mergency B oard T o I nves ­
tigate a D ispute  B etween  the S outh ­
ern P acific C ompany (P acific L ines ) 
and Certain of I ts E mployees
Whereas a dispute exists between the 
Southern Pacific C o m p a n y  (Pacific 
Lines), a carrier, and certain of its em­
ployees represented by the Brotherhood 
of Locomotive Firemen and Enginemen, 
a labor organization; and 
Whereas this dispute has not hereto­
fore been adjusted under the provisions 
of the Railway Labor Act, as amended; 
and
Whereas this dispute, in the judgment 
of the National Mediation Board, threat­
ens substantially to interrupt, interstate 
commerce to a degree such as to deprive 
a large section of the country of essen­
tial transportation service;
NOW, THEREFORE, by virtue of the 
authority vested in me by section 10 of 
the Railway Labor Act, as amended (45 
U. S. C. 160), I hereby create a board 
of three members, to be appointed by 
me, to investigate the said dispute. No 
member of the said board shall be pecu­
niarily or otherwise interested in any 
organization of employees or any carrier.
The board shall report its findings to 
the President with respect to the said 
dispute within thirty days from the date 
of this order.
As provided by section 10 of the Rail­
way Labor Act, as amended, from this 
date and for thirty days after the board 
has made its report to the President, 
no change, except by agreement, shall 
be made by the Southern Pacific Com­
pany (Pacific Lines) or its employees in 
the conditions out of which the said dis­
pute arose.
H arry S. T ruman 
The W hite H ouse , '
March 30,1949.

EXECUTIVE ORDER 10038
C reating  an E m ergency  B oard to  
I nvestigate  D is p u t e B et w e e n th e 
Carriers  R epresented  by  th e E ast­
ern  C arriers ’ C onference  C o m m it t e e, 
W estern  C arriers ’ C onference  C om ­
m itte e and S outheastern  C arriers ’ 
C onference  C o m m ittee, and C ertain  
o f T h e ir E m pl o y ee s
WHEREAS a dispute exists between 
the carriers represented by the Eastern 
Carriers’  Conference Committee, West­
ern Carriers’  Conference Committee and 
Southeastern Carriers’  Conference Com­
mittee, and certain of their employees 
represented by the Brotherhood of Loco­
motive Firemen and Enginemen, a labor 
organization; and
WHEREAS this dispute has not here­
tofore been adjusted under the pro­
visions of the Railway Labor Act, as 
amended; and
WHEREAS this dispute, in the judg­
ment of the National Mediation Board, 
threatens substantially to interrupt in­
terstate commerce to a degree such as to 
deprive the country of essential trans­
portation service:
NOW, THEREFORE, by virtue of the 
authority vested in me by section 10 of 
the Railway Labor Act, as amended (45 
U. S. C. 160), I hereby create a board of 
three members, to be appointed by me, 
to investigate said dispute. No member 
of the said board shall be pecuniarily 
or otherwise interested in any organiza­
tion of railway employees or any carrier.
The board shall report its findings to 
the President with respect to the said 
dispute within thirty days from the date 
of this order.
As provided by section 10 of the Rail­
way Labor Act, as amended, from this 
date and for thirty days after the Board 
has made its report to the President, no 
change, except by agreement, shall be 
made by the carriers represented by the 
Eastern Carriers’  Conference Commit­
tee, Western Carriers’  Committee and 
Southeastern Carriers’  Conference Com­
mittee, or their employees in the con­
ditions out of which the said dispute 
arose.
H arry  S. T ruman
T h e W h it e H ouse ,
February 15, 1949.
[F. R. Doc. 49-1266; Filed, Feb. 15, 1949;
11:47 a. in-.]

Washington, Tuesday,tApril 12, 1949
TITLE 3— THE PRESIDENT
EXECUTIVE ORDER 10050
Creating an E mergency B oard to I nves­
tigate a D ispute  B etween  the R ail­
way Express Agency, Inc., and Certain 
of I ts E mployees
WHEREAS a dispute exists between  
the Railway Express Agency, Inc., a car­
rier, and certain of its employees repre­
sented by the Brotherhood of Railway  
and Steamship Clerks, Freight Handlers,  
Express and Station Employes, a labor  
organization; and
WHEREAS this dispute has not hereto­
fore been adjusted under the provisions  
of the Railway Labor Act, as amended;  
and
WHEREAS this dispute, in the judg­
ment of the National Mediation Board,  
threatens substantially to interrupt in­
terstate commerce to a degree such, as to  
deprive a large section of the country of  
essential transportation service;
NOW, THEREFORE, by virtue of the  
authority vested in me by section 10 of  
the Railway Labor Act, as amended (45  
U. S. C. 160), I hereby create a board of  
three members, to be appointed by me, to  
investigate said dispute. No member of  
the said board shall be pecuniarily or  
otherwise interested in any organization^  
of employees or any carrier.
The board shall report its findings to  
the President with respect to the said  
dispute within thirty days from the date  
of this order.
As provided by section 10 of the Rail­
way Labor Act, as amended, from this  
date and for thirty days after the board  
has made its report to the President, no  
change, except by agreement, shall be  
made by the Railway Express Agency,  
Inc., or its employees in the conditions  
out of which the said dispute arose.
Harry S. T ruman
The W hite  H ouse,
April 9, 1949.

Washington, Saturdayr  July 9, 1949
TITLE 3— THE PRESIDENT
EXECUTIVE ORDER 10067
C reating  an  E m ergency  B oard to I n v e s­
tigate  a D is p u t e B e t w e e n th e M i s­
so u r i P acific  R ailroad  C o m pa n y and

WHEREAS a dispute exists between 
the Missouri Pacific Railroad Company, 
a carrier, and certain of its employees 
represented by the Brotherhood of Loco­
motive Engineers, the Brotherhood of 
Locomotive Firemen and Enginemen, 
the Order of Railway Conductors, and 
the Brotherhood of Railroad Trainmen, 
labor organizations; and 
WHEREAS this dispute has not here­
tofore been adjusted under the provi­
sions of the Railway Labor Act, as 
amendéd; and
WHEREAS this dispute, in the judg­
ment of the National Mediation Board, 
threatens substantially to interrupt in­
terstate commerce within several States 
to a degree such as to deprive a large 
portion of the country of essential trans­
portation service:
NOW, THEREFORE, by virtue of the 
authority vested in me by section 10 of 
the Railway Labor Act, as amended (45 
U. S. C. 160), I hereby create a board of 
three members, to be appointed by me, 
to investigate the said dispute. No mem­
ber of the said board shall be pecuniarily 
or otherwise interested in any organiza­
tion of railway employees or any carrier.
The board shall report its findings to 
the President with respect to the said 
dispute within thirty days from the date 
of this order.
As provided by section 10 of the Rail­
way Labor Act, as amended, from this 
date and for thirty days after the board 
has made its report to the President, no 
change, except by agreement, shall be 
made by the Missouri Pacific Railroad 
Company or its employees in the condi­
tions out of which the said dispute arose.
H arry  S . T rum an 
T h e W h it e H o u se,
July 8, 1949.
[P. R. Doc. 49-5649; Piled, July 8, 1949;  
12:17 p. m .]

AND WEIGHT CLASSES FOR WHOLESALE  
GRADES FOR SHELL EG G S
On January 28,1949, notice of proposed 
rule making was published fn the F ed ­
eral  R egister  (14 F. R. 382) regarding 
the proposed issuance of United States 
Specifications and Weight Classes for 
Wholesale Grades for Shell Eggs. After 
consideration of all relevant matters pre­
sented, including the proposals set forth 
in the aforesaid notice, and pursuant to 
the authority contained in the Depart­
ment of Agriculture Appropriation Act, 
1949 (Public Law 712, 80th Congress, 2d 
Sess., approved June 19, 1948) : It is  
hereby ordered,  That the United States 
Specifications and Weight Classes for 
Wholesale Grades for Shell Eggs shall, 
30 days from date of publication hereof 
in the F ederal  R eg iste r, be as set forth 
below and shall thereupon supersede the 
Tentative U. S. Specifications and Weight 
Classes for Wholesale Grades for Shell 
Eggs that were approved September 17, 
1947. The United States specifications 
for wholesale grades for shell eggs are 
based upon the United States Standards 
for Quality of Individual Shell Eggs 
(7 CFR 42.1 et seq.).

Truman at The White House on October 18, 1948. I read the whole order; the representation is the machine-extracted text layer of the official PDF, and I did not inspect the page images. End: express revocation by Executive Order 12553 on 1986-02-25, under the owner ruling of 2026-10-21 on report deadlines (option C): a report deadline and a spent standstill are not ends. Prior research: a filed claim for this order exists (research/readiness/claims/claude-2026-10-31-apollo-g03.jsonl, line 11) and carries an independent AI whole-case review, but its reasoning described Executive Order 10015 as an amendment without recording it as a later event with a source; this version records it from the Federal Register text of Executive Order 10015 and keeps the end date. Searches: current-law:usc, current-law:ecfr and later-events:ofr_catalog, each marked complete with no coverage warning and no scope note. The U.S. Code and eCFR searches found no hit naming this order; the Federal Register catalog search found the two instruments recorded here (the amendment of November 5, 1948 and the revocation of February 25, 1986).\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"The order contains no fixed end date and no termination clause. Its commands are a bar on interested board members, a requirement that the board report within thirty days from the date of the order (due by November 17, 1948), and a standstill that runs from the date of the order until thirty days after the report. The report deadline and the standstill are spent facts, not ends, under the owner ruling of 2026-10-21 on report deadlines, and neither is claimed as the end. A held lead copy of the board's own report (a text of PDF pages 1-8 of 70 from the National Mediation Board site, graded held_text_lead, a grade the claim format does not accept as a cited source) carries the date December 17, 1948 on its title page and states that the parties stipulated on the record to extend the time for the board to submit its report to the President until December 17; thirty days after December 17, 1948 is January 16, 1949. These dates are leads and are not recorded as later events, because no claim-grade source and no reviewed factual-event record for the report is held for this order; they are also not claimed as an end, and the clause is described only as functionally spent. The dossier chain, the Federal Register catalog disposition note for this order (amended by Executive Order 10015 of November 5, 1948; revoked by Executive Order 12553 of February 25, 1986) and my reading of the held texts of Executive Order 10015 and Executive Order 12553 show no revoking, superseding or terminating instrument before 1986-02-25. Executive Order 10015 is an amendment of the attached lists and is recorded as a later event, not as an end.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"Each command (create the board, bar interested members, require the report, freeze conditions) depends on this order and on the dispute it created; none is a separate delegation, continuing regulation or standing rule. The standstill sentence applies section 10 of the Railway Labor Act (45 U.S.C. 160); the statute is a separate source of law that an Executive order does not revoke, but the periods it measures here were set by this order's creation of the board, and no held claim-grade source shows them still running when the order was revoked.\",\"savings_check\":\"Executive Order 12553 was read in full: it consists of one revoking sentence, a table of the revoked orders by number and subject, an editorial note and the signature block, and it contains no saving or transition provision. No residual effect of this order is shown to depend on its continued force. Savings state none_after_bounded_search; section 10 of the Railway Labor Act continues independently as stated in the independent-command check.\",\"scope_check\":\"One clause is inventoried: the operative text from the order heading through the standstill sentence (the recitals, the creation of the board of three members, the bar on interested members, the thirty-day report requirement and the standstill). The signature, the date line and the attached List A and List B follow it. The recitals incorporate the lists by reference to identify the carriers and the railway labor organizations, and the lists contain no command; Executive Order 10015 later amended them. CASPER, WYO.? Murch 10,1949. 
THE PRESIDENT, 
The White House. 
MR. PRESIDENT : The Emergency Board appointed by you January 
24, 1949, under your Executive Order 10029 dated January 19, 1949, 
and extended 30 days by your order dated 
February 11, 1949, under 
section 10 of the Railway Labor Act, as amended, to investigate an 
~madjusted 
dispute between the Northwest 
Airlines, 
Inc., 
and certain 
of its employees represented by International Association of Ma- 
chinists, has the 
honor 
to submit herewith its report. 
Respectfully 
submitted. 
HAI~RY H. SCHWARTZ? Chairman. 
AARON HORVITZ, Me772 her. 
XOBERT 0. BOYD, Me772 bey.

S. C. 160), I hereby create a board of \nthree members, to be appointed by me, \nto investigate the said dispute. No mem­\nber of the said board shall be pecuniarily \nor otherwise interested in any organiza­\ntion of employees or any carrier.\nThe board shall report its findings to \nthe President with respect to the said \ndispute within thirty days from the date \nof this order.\nAs provided by section 10 of the Rail­\nway Labor Act, as amended, from this \ndate and for thirty days after the board \nhas made its report to the President, no \nchange, except by agreement, shall be \nmade by the Northwest Airlines, Inc., or \nits employees in the conditions out of \nwhich the said dispute arose."},"id":"NA:codex-series-10029-report-record-v1","instrument_id":"EO:10029","legal_effect":"unresolved","producer":"codex-nmb-series-20261025","question_key":"NQ:c3ae6b7a08371900b3a6a90cd5ae6cb0dd5a369e85147c44fffda54802a821d3","schema":"norton-application/v1","scope_id":"EO:10029#whole","state":"ai_reviewed"},"application_review":{"checked_clause_span":{"char_end":2142,"char_start":631,"path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_10029.txt"},"checked_evidence_ids":["NE:codex-series-10029-transmittal-v1"],"checks_reviewed":{"applicability":"Exact EO/date/carrier/union and board report/annual case identities must be read together. {"factual_event_ids":[],"factual_obligations":[{"application":{"answer_id":"NFA:astra-pullman-10032-transmittal-v1","checks":{},"clause_evidence":{"char_end":2224,"char_start":580,"id":"CLAUSES:EO:10032#whole","instrument_id":"EO:10032","kind":"whole","label":"sole paragraph","pdf_page":1,"scope_id":"EO:10032#whole","source":{"filing_date":null,"location_confidence":"high","location_verified":false,"page_or_item":"14 FR 429, PDF pages 1-3","requires_page_check":false,"sha256":"d932782219e3f18165b8b34a840324970cee8be32ab592cffd5aa05774209e6d","signing_date":"1949-01-28","text_path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_10032.txt","text_sha256":"49c4d157b85005a24041379993faf400dc6ee9078f9f3d5f72f3e7fab7b2b3f6","url":"https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/FR-1949-02-01/pdf/FR-1949-02-01.pdf"},"text":"EXECUTIVE ORDER 10032\nCreating an E mergency B oard to I nves­\ntigate a D ispute  B etween  the A kron, \nCanton &  Youngstown  R ailroad C om­\npany  and O ther C arriers, and C ertain \nof T heir E mployees\nWHEREAS a dispute exists between \nthe Akron, Canton ty  Youngstown Rail­\nroad Company and certain other carriers \ndesignated in the list attached hereto \nand made a part hereof, and certain of \ntheir employees represented by the \nBrotherhood of Locomotive Engineers, a \nlabor organization; and \nWHEREAS this dispute has not here-' \ntofore been adjusted under the provi­\nsions of the Railway Labor Act, as \namended; and\nWHEREAS this dispute, in the judg­\nement of the National Mediation Board, \nthreatens substantially* to interrupt in­\nterstate commerce to a degree such as \nto deprive the country of essential trans­\nportation service:\nNOW, THEREFORE, by virtue of the \nauthority vested in me by section 10 of \nthe Railway Labor Act, as amended (45 \nU. S. C. 160), I hereby create a board \nof three members, to be appointed by \nme, to investigate the said dispute. No \nmember of the said board shall be pe­\ncuniarily or otherwise interested in any \norganization of employees or any carrier.\nThe board shall report its findings to \nthe President with respect to the said \ndispute within thirty days from the date of this order.\nAs provided by section 10 of the Rail­\nway Labor Act, as amended, from this \ndate and for thirty days after the board \nhas made its report to the President, no \nchange, except by agreement, shall be \nmade by any of the carriers involved or \nits employees in the conditions out of \nwhich the said dispute arose."},"id":"NA:astra-pullman-10032-transmittal-v1","instrument_id":"EO:10032","legal_effect":"unresolved","producer":"astra-report-bridge-producer","question_key":"NQ:96aecd6e2765dd44cd79b047e3f073d0b5b1e4acc79c578b2a37e19a2f110a7e","schema":"norton-application/v1","scope_id":"EO:10032#whole","state":"candidate"},"application_review":null,"candidate_evidence_ids":[],"citable_factual_events":[],"factual_event_errors":[],"factual_review_status":"candidate","input_origin":"jobs/norton/output/FRAGMENTS.jsonl","legal_effect":"unresolved","machine_lead":true,"question":{"edition_required":"PEB-068 report historical transmittal letter (NMB-held scan)","entity_id":"Presidential Emergency Board 68 established by EO:10032 on 1949-01-28","event_kind":"other_event","interval_end":"1986-02-25","interval_start":"1949-01-28","object":"PEB 68 final report transmittal letter","origin":"owner_seeded","origin_reference":"Owner leverage-first campaign, 2026-10-22; report-deadline ruling 2026-10-21","proposition":"What date does the identified report transmittal letter bear? Inventory: one whole-order clause, the dossier span at offsets 580 to 2224 of the held text, from the order's heading through its last operative sentence (the sentence barring changes in conditions). Text: The order recites that a dispute exists between the Akron, Canton & Youngstown Railroad Company and certain other carriers designated in a list attached to the order, and certain of their employees represented by the Brotherhood of Locomotive Engineers, a labor organization. Acting under section 10 of the Railway Labor Act, as amended (45 U.S.C. 160), it creates a board of three members, to be appointed by the President, to investigate the dispute; it bars any board member from being pecuniarily or otherwise interested in any organization of employees or any carrier; it directs the board to report its findings to the President within thirty days from the date of the order; and, as provided by section 10, it bars any of the carriers involved or its employees from changing, except by agreement, the conditions out of which the dispute arose, from the date of the order until thirty days after the board has made its report to the President. The list of carriers (Eastern, Western and Southeastern regions) follows the signature block in the held text; it identifies the carriers and is not a separate command. Ending: Executive Order 12553 of February 25, 1986 (51 FR 7237, held govinfo text layer) has an enacting sentence reading, in part, \"it is hereby ordered that the following Executive Orders, and\" and, on the next line, \"any amendments thereto, are revoked:\" (the table row for 10032 describes creating an emergency board to investigate a railroad labor dispute). The enacting sentence has no effective-date wording and no exception or saving clause, so the first end is dated on signing, 1986-02-25 (a reading disclosed for the reviewer; the March 3, 1986 printing date is not used). Report: the held National Mediation Board copy of the board's report (jobs/source-ladder/output/texts/queue_EO_10032_Actual_report_date_of_the_board_created_by_EO_10032_Presidential_Emergency_Board_Rail_cf5124cbbf.txt, a held text lead and not an authenticated original) has a cover page dated April 11, 1949 and a letter of transmittal to the President dated Washington, D.C., April 11, 1949; the board states that the President approved, on February 19, 1949, an extension permitting it to file its report not later than April 13, 1949. The report deadline and the section 10 standstill are spent facts and not ends under the owner ruling of 2026-10-21 (option C): taking April 11, 1949 as the date the board made its report, the order was functionally spent on April 11, 1949 and the thirty-day period after the report ran to May 11, 1949 (a computed date, thirty days after that cover or transmittal date, not a date shown in a source); the order stayed on the books until the express revocation. The dossier has no citable record covering the report's date line, so the report date is stated here and is not entered as a later event. Prior research: the packet reports no prior claim or review for this order. Searches: current-law:usc, current-law:ecfr and later-events:ofr_catalog, each marked complete in the packet with no coverage warning; they are the packet's machine searches. Extension of the report deadline: the held National Mediation Board report copy (a held text lead, not an authenticated original, so it cannot be registered as a later-event source in this sheet) states that the President approved on February 19, 1949 an extension of the report time to not later than April 13, 1949; the extension moves the report deadline and is not an end, and it is disclosed here and not entered as a later event.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Earlier-end check: the order's own terms contain a thirty-day report deadline and a standstill running until thirty days after the report, neither of which is an end; no stated fixed end date or termination clause appears in the held text. The packet's bounded number search of 33,118 held texts (clause drafts excluded) found citations only in this order's own printing, the National Mediation Board report copy. The dossier's chain of later events names only Executive Order 12553 as acting on this order, and the three selected machine searches show no revoking, superseding or terminating instrument before 1986-02-25.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"Independent commands: the creation of the board, the bar on interested members, the report deadline, the standstill all concern the single dispute the order addressed; none is a separate delegation, standing rule or continuing regulation. The standstill sentence restates section 10 of the Railway Labor Act, which continues on its own statutory force; the period it measures here was set by this order's creation of the board and nothing held shows it still running.\",\"savings_check\":\"Savings: Executive Order 12553 contains no saving clause (the enacting sentence is followed only by the table). The order's one-time commands (creating the board, the report, the status quo period) were exhausted by performance or lapse, section 10 of the Railway Labor Act continues independently and needs no support from this order, and no surviving appointment, transfer or right is shown in any held source. State none_after_bounded_search.\",\"scope_check\":\"Scope: Executive Order 12553 revokes the order by number, the table entry matches the order's subject, and the enacting sentence carries no exception, so the revocation reaches the whole order and every clause inventoried here.\"}","review_dependency_issues":[],"reviews":[],"scopes":[{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:10032#whole"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:10032#whole"}],"source_pins":[{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761","path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_12553.txt","sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761","state":"match"},"sha256":"945348afbba863ae7dc1fbe7df8759682fe1733962734a9255a1062c0561048c","text_path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_12553.txt","text_sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761"},{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"49c4d157b85005a24041379993faf400dc6ee9078f9f3d5f72f3e7fab7b2b3f6","path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_10032.txt","sha256":"49c4d157b85005a24041379993faf400dc6ee9078f9f3d5f72f3e7fab7b2b3f6","state":"match"},"sha256":"d932782219e3f18165b8b34a840324970cee8be32ab592cffd5aa05774209e6d","text_path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_10032.txt","text_sha256":"49c4d157b85005a24041379993faf400dc6ee9078f9f3d5f72f3e7fab7b2b3f6"}]}]}

Inventory: one whole-order clause, the dossier span at offsets 4470 to 6429 of the held text, from the order's heading through its last operative sentence (the sentence barring changes in conditions). Text: The order recites that a dispute exists between the carriers represented by the Eastern Carriers' Conference Committee, the Western Carriers' Conference Committee and the Southeastern Carriers' Conference Committee, and certain of their employees represented by the Brotherhood of Locomotive Firemen and Enginemen, a labor organization. Acting under section 10 of the Railway Labor Act, as amended (45 U.S.C. 160), it creates a board of three members, to be appointed by the President, to investigate the dispute; it bars any board member from being pecuniarily or otherwise interested in any organization of railway employees or any carrier; it directs the board to report its findings to the President within thirty days from the date of the order; and, as provided by section 10, it bars the carriers represented by those three conference committees or their employees from changing, except by agreement, the conditions out of which the dispute arose, from the date of the order until thirty days after the board has made its report to the President. Ending: Executive Order 12553 of February 25, 1986 (51 FR 7237, held govinfo text layer) has an enacting sentence reading, in part, \"it is hereby ordered that the following Executive Orders, and\" and, on the next line, \"any amendments thereto, are revoked:\" (the table row for 10038 describes creating an emergency board to investigate a railroad labor dispute). The enacting sentence has no effective-date wording and no exception or saving clause, so the first end is dated on signing, 1986-02-25 (a reading disclosed for the reviewer; the March 3, 1986 printing date is not used). Report: the held National Mediation Board copy of the board's report (jobs/source-ladder/output/texts/queue_EO_10038_Actual_report_date_of_the_board_created_by_EO_10038_Presidential_Emergency_Board_Rail_52bfb598d4.txt, a held text lead and not an authenticated original) has a cover page dated September 19, 1949; the board states that the President approved extension requests on February 28, 1949 and on July 27, 1949, the second extending its time to September 19, 1949. The report deadline and the section 10 standstill are spent facts and not ends under the owner ruling of 2026-10-21 (option C): taking September 19, 1949 as the date the board made its report, the order was functionally spent on September 19, 1949 and the thirty-day period after the report ran to October 19, 1949 (a computed date, thirty days after that cover or transmittal date, not a date shown in a source); the order stayed on the books until the express revocation. The dossier has no citable record covering the report's date line, so the report date is stated here and is not entered as a later event. Prior research: the packet reports no prior claim or review for this order. Searches: current-law:usc, current-law:ecfr and later-events:ofr_catalog, each marked complete in the packet with no coverage warning; they are the packet's machine searches.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Earlier-end check: the order's own terms contain a thirty-day report deadline and a standstill running until thirty days after the report, neither of which is an end; no stated fixed end date or termination clause appears in the held text. The packet's bounded number search of 33,118 held texts (clause drafts excluded) found citations only in this order's own printing, the National Mediation Board report copy. The dossier's chain of later events names only Executive Order 12553 as acting on this order, and the three selected machine searches show no revoking, superseding or terminating instrument before 1986-02-25.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"Independent commands: the creation of the board, the bar on interested members, the report deadline, the standstill all concern the single dispute the order addressed; none is a separate delegation, standing rule or continuing regulation. The standstill sentence restates section 10 of the Railway Labor Act, which continues on its own statutory force; the period it measures here was set by this order's creation of the board and nothing held shows it still running.\",\"savings_check\":\"Savings: Executive Order 12553 contains no saving clause (the enacting sentence is followed only by the table). The order's one-time commands (creating the board, the report, the status quo period) were exhausted by performance or lapse, section 10 of the Railway Labor Act continues independently and needs no support from this order, and no surviving appointment, transfer or right is shown in any held source. State none_after_bounded_search.\",\"scope_check\":\"Scope: Executive Order 12553 revokes the order by number, the table entry matches the order's subject, and the enacting sentence carries no exception, so the revocation reaches the whole order and every clause inventoried here.\"}","review_dependency_issues":[],"reviews":[],"scopes":[{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:10038#whole"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:10038#whole"}],"source_pins":[{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761","path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_12553.txt","sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761","state":"match"},"sha256":"945348afbba863ae7dc1fbe7df8759682fe1733962734a9255a1062c0561048c","text_path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_12553.txt","text_sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761"},{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"ee7ae6cba954e57287182eac031dfad2043e31aef3630fe1854e74505b892325","path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_10038.txt","sha256":"ee7ae6cba954e57287182eac031dfad2043e31aef3630fe1854e74505b892325","state":"match"},"sha256":"957b9dde9cfd9acd1ac9b52b6a3a5d8c898bab6f71c368d4fb03a71bfaf5eabe","text_path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_10038.txt","text_sha256":"ee7ae6cba954e57287182eac031dfad2043e31aef3630fe1854e74505b892325"}]}]}

the source URL is the govinfo PDF of the April 1, 1949 issue. Inventory corrected: one clause, offsets 1380 to 2201 of the held text, from the enacting words to the last operative sentence; the dossier's four spans run on past the end of the order into unrelated page text. The order's heading and recitals precede it in the held text and contain no command. Text: The order recites that a dispute exists between the Southern Pacific Company (Pacific Lines), a carrier, and certain of its employees represented by the Brotherhood of Locomotive Firemen and Enginemen, a labor organization. Acting under section 10 of the Railway Labor Act, as amended (45 U.S.C. 160), it creates a board of three members, to be appointed by the President, to investigate the dispute; it bars any board member from being pecuniarily or otherwise interested in any organization of employees or any carrier; it directs the board to report its findings to the President within thirty days from the date of the order; and, as provided by section 10, it bars the Southern Pacific Company (Pacific Lines) or its employees from changing, except by agreement, the conditions out of which the dispute arose, from the date of the order until thirty days after the board has made its report to the President. Ending: Executive Order 12553 of February 25, 1986 (51 FR 7237, held govinfo text layer) has an enacting sentence reading, in part, \"it is hereby ordered that the following Executive Orders, and\" and, on the next line, \"any amendments thereto, are revoked:\" (the table row for 10048 describes creating an emergency board to investigate a railroad labor dispute). The enacting sentence has no effective-date wording and no exception or saving clause, so the first end is dated on signing, 1986-02-25 (a reading disclosed for the reviewer; the March 3, 1986 printing date is not used). Report: the held National Mediation Board copy of the board's report (jobs/source-ladder/output/texts/queue_EO_10048_Actual_report_date_of_the_board_created_by_EO_10048_Presidential_Emergency_Board_Rail_6ee4f2ba5b.txt, a held text lead and not an authenticated original) has a cover page dated April 29, 1949 and a letter of transmittal dated San Francisco, Calif., April 29, 1949. The report deadline and the section 10 standstill are spent facts and not ends under the owner ruling of 2026-10-21 (option C): taking April 29, 1949 as the date the board made its report, the order was functionally spent on April 29, 1949 and the thirty-day period after the report ran to May 29, 1949 (a computed date, thirty days after that cover or transmittal date, not a date shown in a source); the order stayed on the books until the express revocation. The dossier has no citable record covering the report's date line, so the report date is stated here and is not entered as a later event. Prior research: the packet reports no prior claim or review for this order. Searches: current-law:usc, current-law:ecfr and later-events:ofr_catalog, each marked complete in the packet with no coverage warning; they are the packet's machine searches.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Earlier-end check: the order's own terms contain a thirty-day report deadline and a standstill running until thirty days after the report, neither of which is an end; no stated fixed end date or termination clause appears in the held text. The packet's bounded number search of 33,118 held texts (clause drafts excluded) found citations only in this order's own printing, the National Mediation Board report copy. The dossier's chain of later events names only Executive Order 12553 as acting on this order, and the three selected machine searches show no revoking, superseding or terminating instrument before 1986-02-25.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"Independent commands: the creation of the board, the bar on interested members, the report deadline, the standstill all concern the single dispute the order addressed; none is a separate delegation, standing rule or continuing regulation. The standstill sentence restates section 10 of the Railway Labor Act, which continues on its own statutory force; the period it measures here was set by this order's creation of the board and nothing held shows it still running.\",\"savings_check\":\"Savings: Executive Order 12553 contains no saving clause (the enacting sentence is followed only by the table). The order's one-time commands (creating the board, the report, the status quo period) were exhausted by performance or lapse, section 10 of the Railway Labor Act continues independently and needs no support from this order, and no surviving appointment, transfer or right is shown in any held source. State none_after_bounded_search.\",\"scope_check\":\"Scope: Executive Order 12553 revokes the order by number, the table entry matches the order's subject, and the enacting sentence carries no exception, so the revocation reaches the whole order and every clause inventoried here.\"}","review_dependency_issues":[],"reviews":[],"scopes":[{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:10048#whole"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:10048#whole"}],"source_pins":[{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761","path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_12553.txt","sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761","state":"match"},"sha256":"945348afbba863ae7dc1fbe7df8759682fe1733962734a9255a1062c0561048c","text_path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_12553.txt","text_sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761"},{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"532909eb38bcb8ae56235f5af4721bb06a7b8248b30426a7ac5da3bdddd9e1d5","path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_10048.txt","sha256":"532909eb38bcb8ae56235f5af4721bb06a7b8248b30426a7ac5da3bdddd9e1d5","state":"match"},"sha256":"276e79fa9314ec263ca56e25afb36d55bfac4a7fc2be2183f48751f5c2dedb88","text_path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_10048.txt","text_sha256":"532909eb38bcb8ae56235f5af4721bb06a7b8248b30426a7ac5da3bdddd9e1d5"}]}]}

S. C. 160), I hereby create a board of \nthree members, to be appointed by me, \nto investigate the said dispute. No \nmember of the said board shall be pe­\ncuniarily or otherwise interested in any \norganization of railway employees or any \ncarrier.\nThe board shall report its findings to \nthe President with respect to the said \ndispute within thirty days from the date \nof this order.\nAs provided by section 10 of the Rail­\nway Labor Act, as amended, from this \ndate and for thirty days after the board \nhas made its report to the President, no \nchange, except by agreement, shall be \nmade by the Union Railroad Company \n(Pittsburgh) orbits employees in the con­\nditions out of which the said dispute \narose."},"id":"NA:astra-pullman-10056-transmittal-v1","instrument_id":"EO:10056","legal_effect":"unresolved","producer":"astra-report-bridge-producer","question_key":"NQ:b5f93f3a50e883277a10687e7cfe5a41bb7424b8fee07e9752d646d7d3b63d34","schema":"norton-application/v1","scope_id":"EO:10056#whole","state":"candidate"},"application_review":null,"candidate_evidence_ids":[],"citable_factual_events":[],"factual_event_errors":[],"factual_review_status":"candidate","input_origin":"jobs/norton/output/FRAGMENTS.jsonl","legal_effect":"unresolved","machine_lead":true,"question":{"edition_required":"PEB-075 report historical transmittal letter (NMB-held scan)","entity_id":"Presidential Emergency Board 75 established by EO:10056 on 1949-05-12","event_kind":"other_event","interval_end":"1986-02-25","interval_start":"1949-05-12","object":"PEB 75 final report transmittal letter","origin":"owner_seeded","origin_reference":"Owner leverage-first campaign, 2026-10-22; report-deadline ruling 2026-10-21","proposition":"What date does the identified report transmittal letter bear? Searches: current-law:usc, current-law:ecfr, later-events:ofr_catalog, all complete with no coverage warning.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"The order itself contains no fixed end date and no termination clause. Its report deadline (June 11, 1949, thirty days after signing on May 12, 1949) is a deadline, not an end, and the standstill that runs thirty days after the report is likewise a spent fact under the 2026-10-21 owner ruling; neither is claimed as the end. The actual report date and standstill expiry are not recorded as later events because no reviewed factual-event record for this order's report is in the packet; any report text found in the repository is unreviewed context. The dossier's chain of later events, its three selected machine searches (U.S. Code, eCFR, Federal Register catalog dispositions, each marked complete) and my reading of the held text of Executive Order 12553 show no revoking, superseding or terminating instrument before 1986-02-25.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"Each command (create the board, bar interested members, require the report, freeze conditions) depends on the order and on the dispute it created; none is a separate delegation, continuing regulation or standing rule. The standstill sentence restates section 10 of the Railway Labor Act (45 U.S.C. 160); that statute is an independent source of duty and is untouched by the revocation, but the periods it measures here were set by this order's creation of the board and nothing held shows them still running.\",\"savings_check\":\"Executive Order 12553 was read in full and contains no saving clause. The residual consequences of the order (the board's investigation and report, any settlement of the dispute) are not shown to depend on its continued force. Savings state none_after_bounded_search; section 10 of the Railway Labor Act continues independently as stated in the independent-command check.\",\"scope_check\":\"One clause is inventoried: the whole operative text from the order heading through the standstill sentence (recitals, creation of the board, the bar on interested members, the thirty-day report requirement and the standstill).
